3. What is the difference between “ETA Expiry date” and “Visa
Expiry Date”?
Ans: ETA expiry date is the last date allowing bearer access into
India. Visa Expiry Date is the last date permitted for the stay of
foreigner in India subjected to any stay stipulation applicable.

5. What is the difference between “Visa validity/Visa expiry
date” and “Stay stipulation”?
Ans: Visa validity or Visa expiry date refers to the validity period
during which the foreigner can stay in India on the strength of Visa
subject to stay stipulations.
Stay stipulations are the rules which determine the number of days a
foreigner may be permitted to stay in India on the strength of Visa.
Stay stipulations are of two kinds 1) Continuous stay in India from
the date of last arrival. 2) Total Aggregate stay in India during the
calendar year.

FAQ (Frequently Asked Questions)
1.What is an ETA?
Ans: ETA is an Electronic Travel Authorization. It allows the bearer
access into the issuing country (India). ETA is NOT eVisa. When the
bearer of ETA is allowed entry into India by the Immigration Officer,
it becomes eVisa. eVisa will be stamped on the Passport of the
foreigner at the designated ICPs on production of valid ETA.

FAQ (Frequently Asked Questions)
1.What is an ETA?
Ans: ETA is an Electronic Travel Authorization. It allows the bearer
access into the issuing country (India). ETA is NOT eVisa. When the
bearer of ETA is allowed entry into India by the Immigration Officer,
it becomes eVisa. eVisa will be stamped on the Passport of the
foreigner at the designated ICPs on production of valid ETA.

🧑 You: What is biomis

🤖 AI:
BIOMIS (Biodiversity Management Information System) is a web-enabled unique repository of biodiversity information developed by NIC (National Informatics Centre) for the Kerala State Biodiversity Board. It serves NBA, State Biodiversity Boards (SBB), Union Territory Biodiversity Councils (UTBC), Biodiversity Management Committees (BMC), and the public. It provides open and free access to biodiversity information and enables widespread participation by all citizens.

==================================================

Chatbot

🧑 You: What is PBR

🤖 AI:
PBR stands for People's Biodiversity Register. It is a comprehensive database recording people's traditional knowledge and insight of the status, uses, history, ongoing changes, and forces driving changes on the biological diversity resources of their localities. It provides information on current utilization patterns of biodiversity and its economic benefits to local communities. Biodiversity Boards prepare PBRs for each Local Self Government (LSG) body involving local communities.

Q: What is NBA?
A: NBA stands for National Biodiversity Authority. It is the apex body managing

system at the national level, maintains master data, manages the master species
database provided by ZSI and BSI, oversees system governance, and supervises
Access and Benefit Sharing mechanisms.

🧑 You: Table of Content

🤖 AI:
Q: How is the EPBR certificate generated?
A: After SBB/UTBC approval and BMC Meeting approval, the system automatically
generates the EPBR Report and Certificate as per Rule 22(10) of the Biological
Diversity Rules 2004. The certificate is endorsed with resolution number and date,
signed by the BMC Chairman, BMC Secretary, counter-signed by a representative of
NGO or organization involved in the PBR exercise, and counter-signed by the Member
Secretary of SBB or authorized official of SBB.

Q: How do I contact Kerala State Biodiversity Board?
A: Kerala State Biodiversity Board can be contacted at: Address — Jai Nagar,
Krishna Villa, Kuravankonam, Thiruvananthapuram, Kerala 695001. Email:
ksbiodiversity@gmail.com. The BIOMIS portal was developed and is maintained by NIC
(National Informatics Centre), Kerala State Unit, Ministry of Communications and
Information Technology, Government of India.

🧑 You: What is UTBC?

🤖 AI:
UTBC stands for Union Territory Biodiversity Council. It is the Union Territory-level equivalent of the State Biodiversity Board (SBB) and performs all the same functions within its jurisdiction including verification, approval, and publication of EPBRs.

Saved Time:

Chatbot

🧑 You: What is SBB

🤖 AI:
SBB stands for State Biodiversity Board. It is the state-level biodiversity governance body. In BIOMIS, SBB enters state-specific data (soil, vegetation, geoscape, land use), constitutes Verification Officers, reviews and approves EPBR submissions, and publishes the final EPBR. SBB is the primary approving authority at the state level.

Chatbot

🧑 You: role of the NBA Super Admin

🤖 AI:
The NBA Super Admin manages the system at the national level, handles and maintains master data, manages the master species database provided by ZSI and BSI, oversees overall system governance, and supervises ABS mechanisms. Master data created by the NBA Super Admin forms the foundation for all state and district-level entries.

Time Duration: 7.45ms

Saved Time:
2026-05-27 14:22:07


==================================================

Chatbot

🧑 You: full EPBR workflow

🤖 AI:
The EPBR workflow has 6 steps. Step 1 — Data Entry: BMC enters data across all EPBR tabs year-wise; TSG may assist. Step 2 — Submission: BMC officer submits the completed data. Step 3 — Verification: Data moves digitally to the Verification Officer who validates it against actual ground data. Step 4 — Validation Outcome: If not valid, data is returned to BMC with remarks for correction and resubmission. If valid, data is forwarded to SBB/UTBC. Step 5 — Approval: SBB/UTBC reviews and approves. EPBR is placed before the BMC Meeting for formal approval. Once approved, the system generates the EPBR Report and Certificate. Step 6 — Publication: SBB/UTBC publishes the final EPBR and links it to the ABS framework.

Time Duration: 6.76ms

Saved Time:
2026-05-27 14:23:06

🧑 You: What is ABS and how does it relate to EPBR

🤖 AI:
ABS stands for Access and Benefit Sharing. Once the EPBR is published, it is linked to the ABS framework to ensure that communities who are custodians of biological resources receive fair benefits when those resources are accessed or used commercially. The NBA Super Admin oversees the ABS mechanism at the national level.

5. What is the difference between “Visa validity/Visa expiry
date” and “Stay stipulation”?
Ans: Visa validity or Visa expiry date refers to the validity period
during which the foreigner can stay in India on the strength of Visa
subject to stay stipulations.
Stay stipulations are the rules which determine the number of days a
foreigner may be permitted to stay in India on the strength of Visa.
Stay stipulations are of two kinds 1) Continuous stay in India from
the date of last arrival. 2) Total Aggregate stay in India during the
calendar year.

FAQ (Frequently Asked Questions)
1.What is an ETA?
Ans: ETA is an Electronic Travel Authorization. It allows the bearer
access into the issuing country (India). ETA is NOT eVisa. When the
bearer of ETA is allowed entry into India by the Immigration Officer,
it becomes eVisa. eVisa will be stamped on the Passport of the
foreigner at the designated ICPs on production of valid ETA.

🧑 You: master data in BIOMIS include

🤖 AI:
Master data is created and maintained by the NBA Super Admin at the national level. It includes the master species database provided by ZSI and BSI, local body types, district and state data, agency types, and other system-wide reference data. This master data forms the foundation for all state and district-level entries. SBB can enter local names corresponding to master species data.

Expiry Date”?

India. Visa Expiry Date is the last date permitted for the stay of
foreigner in India subjected to any stay stipulation applicable.

5. What is the difference between “Visa validity/Visa expiry
date” and “Stay stipulation”?
Ans: Visa validity or Visa expiry date refers to the validity period
during which the foreigner can stay in India on the strength of Visa
subject to stay stipulations.
Stay stipulations are the rules which determine the number of days a
foreigner may be permitted to stay in India on the strength of Visa.
Stay stipulations are of two kinds 1) Continuous stay in India from
the date of last arrival. 2) Total Aggregate stay in India during the
calendar year.

FAQ (Frequently Asked Questions)
1.What is an ETA?
Ans: ETA is an Electronic Travel Authorization. It allows the bearer
access into the issuing country (India). ETA is NOT eVisa. When the
bearer of ETA is allowed entry into India by the Immigration Officer,
it becomes eVisa. eVisa will be stamped on the Passport of the
foreigner at the designated ICPs on production of valid ETA.

🧑 You: who is tge frro for Kattupalli.

🤖 AI:
1: What is the general rule for foreigners regarding
registration in India?
A: Foreign nationals must register with the Foreigner’s Regional
Registration Office (FRRO) or the Foreigner’s Registration Office (FRO)
based on their visa category, stay duration, and specific
endorsements on their visa.

Q: Who are required to register within 14 days of arrival?
A: Foreigners entering India on the following visa types must register
within 14 days if their visa is valid for more than 180 days:
• Student Visa
• Employment Visa
• Medical Visa
• Missionary Visa

Q: When does a Business Visa holder need to register?
A: A Business Visa holder is required to register if:
• Their continuous stay in India exceeds 180 days.
• Their aggregate (total) stay within a single calendar year
(January to December) exceeds 180 days.

Q: Are there registration requirements for other visa types?
A: Yes. Foreigners on any visa type (excluding those on Official or
Diplomatic passports) must register if their continuous stay on a
single entry or their aggregate stay in a calendar year exceeds 180
days.

Q: What are the specific registration rules for Pakistani
nationals?
A: Pakistani nationals face stricter timelines and must register with
the local FRO within 24 hours of their arrival in India.

Q: Which categories of people are exempt from registration?
A: The following groups do not need to register with the FRRO/FRO:
• Children: Anyone below 12 years of age.
• OCI Cardholders: Overseas Citizens of India are fully exempt.
• Neighbouring Citizens: Citizens of Nepal and Bhutan do not
require a visa or registration.

Q: What happens if a foreigner fails to register?
A: Non-compliance with registration rules is a serious matter and shall
result in legal consequences, which may include fines, restrictions on
future travel, or deportation.

Q: Are there any other rules a foreigner should follow?
A: Yes. All foreigners must strictly adhere to any specific conditions
or stipulations endorsed on their visa at all times.
SPECIAL CATEGORY REGISTRATION (Pakistan, Afghanistan,
Bangladesh)

Q: What are the specific registration rules for Pakistani
nationals?
A: Generally, every Pakistani national entering India must register
with the local authorities within 24 hours of arrival.

Q: Are there any exceptions to the 24-hour rule for Pakistani
nationals?
A: Yes, there are two main exceptions:
• Police Reporting Exemption: Registration is not required if the
visa has an official endorsement stating "Exemption from police
reporting."
• Medical Visas: Pakistani nationals arriving on a Medical Visa are
given a longer window and must register within 7 days of arrival.

Q: How should an Afghan national apply for a stay visa or
residential permit?
A: Afghan nationals who wish to apply for a Stay Visa or Residential
Permit must do so online via the official portal: indianfrro.gov.in.

Q: When is registration mandatory for Bangladeshi nationals?
A: A Bangladeshi national must register with the jurisdictional
FRRO/FRO and obtain a residential permit within 14 days of arrival if
they intend to stay in India for a continuous period exceeding six
months.

Q: What is a Long Term Visa (LTV), and who is eligible?
A: Certain categories of nationals from Afghanistan, Bangladesh, and
Pakistan living in India may be eligible for a Long Term Visa (LTV).
This provides extended residency benefits for eligible individuals.

Q: Where can I find the official guidelines for Long Term Visas
(LTV)?
A: Detailed instructions regarding the grant and extension of LTV
facilities can be found in the Ministry of Home Affairs (MHA)
documentation here: MHA LTV Guidelines.

bearer of ETA is allowed entry into India by the Immigration Officer,
it becomes eVisa. eVisa will be stamped on the Passport of the
foreigner at the designated ICPs on production of valid ETA.

🧑 You: How do I enter EPBR data as a BMC user

🤖 AI:
Login with your BMC credentials. You will see a role-based dashboard showing the status of PBR applications, activities, and new findings. Navigate to the EPBR option in the menu. The system will display your panchayat details based on your assigned role. Fill in data across all the EPBR tabs (General, BMC, Domain Knowledge Holder, Species, Pest, Market, Land Scape, Soil Type, History, Civilization) for the current year. After completing all tabs, submit the data. Contact your TSG or District Admin for help with specific fields.

🧑 You: What is the General tab in EPBR

🤖 AI:
The General tab captures the basic geographical and demographic data of the panchayat including: geographical area, census data (population by male, female, transgender, total), climate information, landscape type, habitat and topography details, and management regime. This is the first tab to fill in the EPBR form.

🧑 You: What is the Domain Knowledge Holder tab in EPBR?

🤖 AI:
The Domain Knowledge Holder tab documents traditional knowledge experts in the panchayat. It captures: name, age, occupation, gender, communication address, area of specialization, domain type (Hakim, Vaidyan, or other), location from which the person accesses biological material, and remarks. This includes traditional healers, Hakims, and Vaidyans.

3. What is the difference between “ETA Expiry date” and “Visa
Expiry Date”?
Ans: ETA expiry date is the last date allowing bearer access into
India. Visa Expiry Date is the last date permitted for the stay of
foreigner in India subjected to any stay stipulation applicable.

3. What activities can I undertake on my Visa?
Ans: You are required to strictly adhere to the purpose of visit
declared while submitting the visa application. Kindly refer to terms
and conditions of your Visa.

5. What is the difference between “Visa validity/Visa expiry
date” and “Stay stipulation”?
Ans: Visa validity or Visa expiry date refers to the validity period
during which the foreigner can stay in India on the strength of Visa
subject to stay stipulations.
Stay stipulations are the rules which determine the number of days a
foreigner may be permitted to stay in India on the strength of Visa.
Stay stipulations are of two kinds 1) Continuous stay in India from
the date of last arrival. 2) Total Aggregate stay in India during the
calendar year.

Ans: Maximum stay by a foreigner in India on an e-Tourist Visa or
paper Tourist Visa or both in one calendar year shall be restricted to
180 days.

8. What is “total aggregate stay in a calendar year” and
“continuous stay”?
Ans: Total Aggregate stay refers to total period of stay of foreigner in
India including multiple visits during the calendar year.
Continuous stay refers to period of stay of foreigner in India since his
last arrival.

12. Can I undertake Tourism while on visit to India on
Business/ Conference/ Medical Visa?
Ans: Foreign nationals who are granted a particular type of visa with
reference to the purpose of visit shall be required to strictly adhere
to the purpose of visit declared while submitting the visa application.
However, a foreign national (other than a Pakistani national) coming
to India on any type of visa will be allowed to avail activities permitted
under Tourist Visa.

FAQ (Frequently Asked Questions)
1.What is an ETA?
Ans: ETA is an Electronic Travel Authorization. It allows the bearer
access into the issuing country (India). ETA is NOT eVisa. When the
bearer of ETA is allowed entry into India by the Immigration Officer,
it becomes eVisa. eVisa will be stamped on the Passport of the
foreigner at the designated ICPs on production of valid ETA.

Q: What is EPBR?
A: EPBR stands for Electronic People's Biodiversity Register. It is the digital
version of the PBR created and managed within the BIOMIS portal. BMCs enter data
across multiple EPBR tabs (General, BMC, Domain Knowledge Holder, Species, Pest
Affecting Crop Details, Market Transaction Details, Land Scape, Soil Type,
History, Civilization) to document local biodiversity information year-wise and
panchayat-wise.

Most FIOs prefer to get an OCI card because it eliminates the need to apply for a visa every time they visit.

Chatbot

🧑 You: eta

🤖 AI:
1.What is an ETA?
Ans: ETA is an Electronic Travel Authorization. It allows the bearer
access into the issuing country (India). ETA is NOT eVisa. When the
bearer of ETA is allowed entry into India by the Immigration Officer,
it becomes eVisa. eVisa will be stamped on the Passport of the
foreigner at the designated ICPs on production of valid ETA.

🧑 You: What is Form III and who needs to fill it?

🤖 AI:
Form III is a mandatory report that every "keeper of accommodation" must submit when a foreign national (including OCI cardholders) stays at their premises. This includes hotels, homestays, hospitals, and even religious institutions.

Chatbot

🧑 You: what is an eta ?

🤖 AI:
Q: When was the Bureau of Immigration (BoI) established?
A: The BoI was established in 1971.

Q: What are the primary functions of the Bureau of Immigration?
A: The BoI is the primary authority in India responsible for enforcing
rules regarding the entry, stay, and exit of foreign nationals. It acts
as the authentic gateway for travel regulations and immigration
services.

Q: Who leads the Bureau of Immigration?
A: The BoI is headed by the Commissioner of Immigration.

Q: Contact details of Commissioner, Bureau of Immigration
A: Name: Ms. I.B. Rani, IPS
Office Address: 7th Floor, Tower 'J', World Trade Centre (WTC),
Nauroji Nagar, New Delhi - 110029
Telephone No: 011-26102622 (Telefax)

Q: Where does the BoI manage immigration facilities?
A: The BoI manages immigration services across India’s International
Check Posts (ICPs) through FRROs (Foreigners Regional Registration
Offices) and CHIOs (Chief Immigration Officers).

Q: What are the Services provided by the BoI for foreign
nationals living in India?
A: The BoI oversees registration services and visa-related formalities
for foreign nationals residing in India, ensuring all activities comply
with the relevant Acts, Rules, and Regulations.

Q: How many Immigration Check Posts (ICPs) are currently
operational in India?
A: There is a total of 114 Immigration Check Posts across India,
covering air, sea, land, rail, and river routes to manage international
traffic.

Q: How many Foreigners Regional Registration Offices
(FRROs) are there under BoI? Where are they located?
A: There are 15 FRROs. They are located in Ahmedabad, Amritsar,
Bangalore, Calicut, Chandigarh, Chennai, Cochin, Delhi, Guwahati,
Hyderabad, Jaipur, Kolkata, Lucknow, Mumbai, and Trivandrum.

Q: How many Chief Immigration Officers are there? Where
are they stationed?
A: There are 24 Chief Immigration Officers functioning at Goa, Jaipur,
Gaya, Varanasi, Nagpur, Pune, Indore, Mangalore, Trichy,
Coimbatore, Bagdogra, Chandigarh, Haridaspur, Kannur, Dera Baba
Nanak, Raxaul, Sonauli, Gede, Munabao, Chitpur, Guwahati, New
Jalpaiguri, Dhamra, and Mopa.

Q: Who handles foreigner registration in areas where there is
no FRRO or Chief Immigration Officer?
A: In locations, where there is no FRRO or Chief Immigration Officer,
the District Superintendents of Police (SPs) act as the Foreigners
Registration Officers (FROs).

A: The BoI was established in 1971.

Q: What are the primary functions of the Bureau of Immigration?
A: The BoI is the primary authority in India responsible for enforcing
rules regarding the entry, stay, and exit of foreign nationals. It acts
as the authentic gateway for travel regulations and immigration
services.

Q: Who leads the Bureau of Immigration?
A: The BoI is headed by the Commissioner of Immigration.

Q: Contact details of Commissioner, Bureau of Immigration
A: Name: Ms. I.B. Rani, IPS
Office Address: 7th Floor, Tower 'J', World Trade Centre (WTC),
Nauroji Nagar, New Delhi - 110029
Telephone No: 011-26102622 (Telefax)

Q: Where does the BoI manage immigration facilities?
A: The BoI manages immigration services across India’s International
Check Posts (ICPs) through FRROs (Foreigners Regional Registration
Offices) and CHIOs (Chief Immigration Officers).

Q: What are the Services provided by the BoI for foreign
nationals living in India?
A: The BoI oversees registration services and visa-related formalities
for foreign nationals residing in India, ensuring all activities comply
with the relevant Acts, Rules, and Regulations.

Q: How many Immigration Check Posts (ICPs) are currently
operational in India?
A: There is a total of 114 Immigration Check Posts across India,
covering air, sea, land, rail, and river routes to manage international
traffic.

Q: How many Foreigners Regional Registration Offices
(FRROs) are there under BoI? Where are they located?
A: There are 15 FRROs. They are located in Ahmedabad, Amritsar,
Bangalore, Calicut, Chandigarh, Chennai, Cochin, Delhi, Guwahati,
Hyderabad, Jaipur, Kolkata, Lucknow, Mumbai, and Trivandrum.

Q: How many Chief Immigration Officers are there? Where
are they stationed?
A: There are 24 Chief Immigration Officers functioning at Goa, Jaipur,
Gaya, Varanasi, Nagpur, Pune, Indore, Mangalore, Trichy,
Coimbatore, Bagdogra, Chandigarh, Haridaspur, Kannur, Dera Baba
Nanak, Raxaul, Sonauli, Gede, Munabao, Chitpur, Guwahati, New
Jalpaiguri, Dhamra, and Mopa.

Q: Who handles foreigner registration in areas where there is
no FRRO or Chief Immigration Officer?
A: In locations, where there is no FRRO or Chief Immigration Officer,
the District Superintendents of Police (SPs) act as the Foreigners
Registration Officers (FROs).

foreigner at the designated ICPs on production of valid ETA.

Q: What is an Overseas Citizen of India (OCI) Cardholder?
A:
An OCI Cardholder is a foreign national who is officially registered by
the Government of India under Section 7A of The Citizenship
(Amendment) Act, 2015. The scheme was created to help the Indian
Diaspora maintain a strong connection with India through benefits
like hassle-free travel and a friendly investment regime.

• Departures from India: Immediately after boarding is closed
or "wheels-off" within India.
• Format required: UNEDIFACT-PAXLST.

==================================================

Chatbot

🧑 You: How do I submit my OCI application

🤖 AI:
1. How do I submit my OCI application? Applications for registration as an OCI Cardholder must be submitted online at https://ociservices.gov.in. You will find detailed information about the specific documents required for your case on that portal.

2. Do I need to visit the FRRO with hard copies? Yes. After you have submitted your application online, you are required to bring the original versions of all uploaded documents to the concerned FRRO for physical verification.

3. Is a personal interview mandatory? If you are applying for a new OCI registration, a personal interview is mandatory. However, children below the age of 12 years are exempt from this requirement.

4. When do I provide my biometrics?
• Applying from abroad: You can provide biometrics at the application stage at the Mission or upon arrival at the Immigration Check Post in India. However, it is highly recommended to do it during the application stage.
• Applying in India: Biometrics must be captured at the application stage at the FRRO.
• Exemptions: Applicants above 70 years or below 12 years of age are exempt from biometric capturing.

5. Can I collect my OCI card through someone else? Yes. You can collect it in person or send an authorized representative with your passport. However, if a representative collects it, you must be physically present in the country where the OCI card is being issued at the time of collection.

6. What happens to the fee if my application is rejected? If registration is not granted, an amount of USD 250 (or local equivalent) will be refunded. Please note that the processing fee (USD 25 or ₹1400 in India) is non-refundable.

7. Does an OCI cardholder lose their original citizenship? No. Registering as an OCI cardholder does not affect your current foreign citizenship.

8. What are the limitations for OCI cardholders in India? While OCI holders enjoy many benefits, there are certain restrictions:
• Voting: You are not entitled to vote in Indian elections.
• Public Office: You cannot hold constitutional posts (President, Vice-President, Judge of the Supreme Court/High Court) or be a member of Parliament or State Legislatures.
• Employment: You are generally not eligible for appointment to public services or government posts.
• Property: You cannot acquire agricultural or plantation properties in India.

9. Can OCI cardholders take entrance exams for Indian colleges? Yes. You are eligible for national entrance tests like NEET, JEE (Mains/Advanced), and others. However, you are not eligible for admission to seats reserved exclusively for Indian citizens.

Time Duration: 25.16ms

Saved Time:
2026-06-02 14:54:17

🧑 You: How do I submit my OCI application

🤖 AI:
You
can find complete details and access online services at the official OCI
portal: https://ociservices.gov.in, or by visiting your nearest Indian
Mission or FRRO.
Q. Is the OCI card a replacement for my national passport?
A: No. Your foreign passport remains your primary travel
document. The OCI card is technically a life-long visa, not a travel
document itself. You must carry both your valid national passport and
your OCI card at all times while traveling to or staying in India.

Chatbot

🧑 You: frro chennai

🤖 AI:
1: What is the general rule for foreigners regarding
registration in India?
A: Foreign nationals must register with the Foreigner’s Regional
Registration Office (FRRO) or the Foreigner’s Registration Office (FRO)
based on their visa category, stay duration, and specific
endorsements on their visa.

Q: Who are required to register within 14 days of arrival?
A: Foreigners entering India on the following visa types must register
within 14 days if their visa is valid for more than 180 days:
• Student Visa
• Employment Visa
• Medical Visa
• Missionary Visa

Q: When does a Business Visa holder need to register?
A: A Business Visa holder is required to register if:
• Their continuous stay in India exceeds 180 days.
• Their aggregate (total) stay within a single calendar year
(January to December) exceeds 180 days.

Q: Are there registration requirements for other visa types?
A: Yes. Foreigners on any visa type (excluding those on Official or
Diplomatic passports) must register if their continuous stay on a
single entry or their aggregate stay in a calendar year exceeds 180
days.

Q: What are the specific registration rules for Pakistani
nationals?
A: Pakistani nationals face stricter timelines and must register with
the local FRO within 24 hours of their arrival in India.

Q: Which categories of people are exempt from registration?
A: The following groups do not need to register with the FRRO/FRO:
• Children: Anyone below 12 years of age.
• OCI Cardholders: Overseas Citizens of India are fully exempt.
• Neighbouring Citizens: Citizens of Nepal and Bhutan do not
require a visa or registration.

Q: What happens if a foreigner fails to register?
A: Non-compliance with registration rules is a serious matter and shall
result in legal consequences, which may include fines, restrictions on
future travel, or deportation.

Q: Are there any other rules a foreigner should follow?
A: Yes. All foreigners must strictly adhere to any specific conditions
or stipulations endorsed on their visa at all times.
SPECIAL CATEGORY REGISTRATION (Pakistan, Afghanistan,
Bangladesh)

Q: What are the specific registration rules for Pakistani
nationals?
A: Generally, every Pakistani national entering India must register
with the local authorities within 24 hours of arrival.

Q: Are there any exceptions to the 24-hour rule for Pakistani
nationals?
A: Yes, there are two main exceptions:
• Police Reporting Exemption: Registration is not required if the
visa has an official endorsement stating "Exemption from police
reporting."
• Medical Visas: Pakistani nationals arriving on a Medical Visa are
given a longer window and must register within 7 days of arrival.

Q: How should an Afghan national apply for a stay visa or
residential permit?
A: Afghan nationals who wish to apply for a Stay Visa or Residential
Permit must do so online via the official portal: indianfrro.gov.in.

Q: When is registration mandatory for Bangladeshi nationals?
A: A Bangladeshi national must register with the jurisdictional
FRRO/FRO and obtain a residential permit within 14 days of arrival if
they intend to stay in India for a continuous period exceeding six
months.

Q: What is a Long Term Visa (LTV), and who is eligible?
A: Certain categories of nationals from Afghanistan, Bangladesh, and
Pakistan living in India may be eligible for a Long Term Visa (LTV).
This provides extended residency benefits for eligible individuals.

Q: Where can I find the official guidelines for Long Term Visas
(LTV)?
A: Detailed instructions regarding the grant and extension of LTV
facilities can be found in the Ministry of Home Affairs (MHA)
documentation here: MHA LTV Guidelines.

Q: Can the Government of India cancel an OCI card?
A: No, the Government of India cannot simply cancel an OCI card. According to the text, OCI registration can be cancelled immediately if:

1. It was obtained through fraud, false representation, or hiding material facts.
2. The holder shows disaffection toward the Constitution of India.
3. The holder violates any provisions of the Citizenship Act.

However, it does not provide a general reason for cancelling an OCI card without these specific circumstances being met.

Chatbot

🧑 You: SEAMEN AND MARITIME CREW FAQ

🤖 AI:
Q: What documents do I need to Sign On or Sign Off at an
Indian seaport?
A: Both Indian and foreign seamen are required to be in possession
of two primary documents:
• A valid Passport.
• A Seaman Book or Continuous Discharge Certificate (CDC).

Q: I am a foreign seaman joining a "Foreign Run" vessel in
India. Do I need a visa?
A: Yes. Foreign seamen joining vessels that operate on international
routes (Foreign Run) must be in possession of a Transit Visa.

Q: What are the visa requirements for joining a "Coastal Run"
vessel?
A: If you are joining a vessel operating within Indian coastal waters,
the requirements depend on your role:
• General Crew: You must have a Business Visa or an X-Misc. Visa
with a specific endorsement to join the vessel (or an OCI card).
• Project Crew: If you are joining specialized vessels such as
Survey, Rig, Cable-laying, Seismic, or Research vessels, you
must hold an Employment Visa.

Q: Can "Supernumeraries" sign off in India?
A: Foreign Supernumeraries are not permitted to Sign Off at an
Indian port unless they are in possession of an appropriate and valid
visa.

Q: How does the Shore Leave Pass (SLP) work?
A: A Shore Leave Pass may be issued to foreign seamen under the
following conditions:
• Daily Validity: From 06:00 hrs to 23:45 hrs.
• Duration: Valid for a maximum of 7 days or until the vessel
departs, whichever happens first.

Q: Are Supernumeraries eligible for shore leave?
A: No. Foreign Supernumeraries are not eligible to receive a Shore
Leave Pass (SLP).

Q: What are the restrictions for Pakistani or Pakistani-origin
crew?
A: Pakistani nationals and crew members of Pakistani origin will not
be issued a Landing Permit if they are arriving in India from any
country other than Pakistan.
BUSINESS VISA (B) IN INDIA: Frequently Asked Questions

Q: When should I apply for a Business Visa instead of other
types?
A: You should apply for a Business Visa if you are visiting India to:
• Establish or explore the possibility of setting up an industrial or
business venture.
• Attend technical meetings, board meetings, or general
discussions.
• Provide business services support or professional consultations.
• Participate in commercial sports events on contract (such as the
IPL, ISL, or IBL), including acting as a coach, where
remuneration is involved.

Q: Can I receive a salary in India while on a Business Visa?
A: No. A foreign national on a Business Visa is not permitted to take
a salary or remuneration in India. If you are intended to be on an
Indian payroll or receive a salary locally, you must apply for an
Employment Visa instead.

Q: Do I need to register with the FRRO?
A: Registration with the FRRO/FRO is required if your aggregate stay
in India (total days spent in the country) exceeds 180 days during a
single calendar year.

Q: What are the requirements for extending a Business Visa?
A: For an extension to be granted, the business must demonstrate a
gross sales or turnover of at least ₹1 crore per annum. This financial
target must be achieved within 4 years of setting up the business or
from the date the initial Business Visa was granted, whichever comes
first.

Q: Can I work for a different company than the one listed on
my visa?
A: No. You must remain associated with the specific company or
organization that provided the visa reference during your application
process.

Q: What happens if my visa extension is denied?
A: If your extension is refused, you must leave India immediately or
upon the expiry of your current visa validity.

Q: Are there any tax obligations?
A: Yes. Every foreign national conducting business in India is required
to comply with all Indian taxation rules and regulations pertaining to
their business activities.
Quick Checklist for Business Visitors
• Primary Purpose: Business set-up, meetings, or commercial
sports.
• Payment Rule: No Indian salary permitted (use Employment
Visa for salary).
• Stay Limit: Register with FRRO if staying over 180 days in a
year.
• Extension Bar: Must meet the ₹1 crore annual turnover
requirement.
Here is the information regarding Medical and Medical Attendant visas
in India, structured into a conversational Q&A format for a chatbot.
Medical Treatment & Attendant Visa FAQ

Q: Who needs to apply for a Medical Visa?
A: Any foreign national who wishes to seek medical treatment in India
must apply for a Medical Visa. This applies to treatments at
established, recognized, or specialized hospitals, as well as accredited
centers providing treatment under Indian systems of medicine (such
as Ayurveda or Yoga).

Q: Can someone accompany me during my treatment?
A: Yes. Family members or associates accompanying a patient must
arrive on a Medical Attendant Visa. This visa is specifically for those
providing support to the primary Medical Visa holder.

Q: How many attendants are allowed to travel with a patient?
A: The number of permitted attendants depends on the patient's
nationality:
• Most Foreign Nationals: A maximum of two attendants are
allowed.
• Bangladesh Nationals: Up to three medical attendants are
permitted.
• Pakistan Nationals: Only one medical attendant is allowed.

Q: What are the registration rules for Pakistan nationals?
A: Pakistan nationals holding either a Medical or Medical Attendant
visa have stricter registration requirements. They must register
themselves with the concerned Foreigners Registration Office (FRO)
within 24 hours of their arrival in India.

Q: Do other foreigners need to register with the FRRO/FRO?
A: Yes, if the visa is valid for a long duration. Foreigners (excluding
Pakistan nationals) whose Medical or Medical Attendant visas are valid
for more than 180 days must register with the FRRO/FRO within 14
days of their arrival.

Q: Can I change my hospital or treatment center once I am in
India?
A: Changing the hospital or place of treatment that was endorsed on
your initial visa is generally not allowed. However, it may be permitted
on a case-to-case basis if you can provide documentary proof that
justifies the change.

Q: What documents are typically required for the application?
A: While you should always check the official portal, you will generally
need:
• An official invitation letter from the recognized Indian
hospital.
• Medical documents establishing the need for treatment.
• Proof of sufficient funds for the duration of the stay and
treatment.
Medical & Attendant Visa Compliance FAQ

Q: If I’m traveling to India for treatment, what kind of visa do
I need, and can someone come with me?
A: If you are the person receiving treatment, you must apply for a
Medical Visa. For those accompanying you—whether they are family
members or caregivers—they will need to apply for a Medical
Attendant Visa. It’s important to remember that the attendant's visa
is linked directly to yours.

Q: Is there a limit on how many people can come with me as
medical attendants?
A: Yes, there are specific limits based on your nationality. For most
international travelers, you are allowed up to two attendants.
However, if you are a citizen of Bangladesh, you are permitted to bring
up to three attendants, while citizens of Pakistan are limited to one
attendant only.

Q: Do I need to register with the Indian authorities once I
arrive?
A: This depends on your nationality and the length of your stay. For
most travelers, registration with the FRRO is only required if your visa
is valid for more than 180 days; in this case, you must register within
14 days of your arrival. However, if you are a citizen of Pakistan,
the rules are stricter: you must register within 24 hours of reaching
India, regardless of your stay duration.

Q: What exactly is a Medical Attendant Visa?
A: Think of it as a secondary visa for your "escort." It is designed for
the person who will be assisting the patient during their medical
journey. Because it is a supportive visa, it usually has the same
validity period as the patient’s primary Medical Visa.

Q: Are there any specific documents the attendant needs to
show?
A: Along with their own application, the attendant usually needs a
letter from the hospital in India that specifically mentions their name
as the designated caregiver for the patient. They may also need to
provide proof of their relationship to the patient.

Q: Can I change my attendants once I am already in India?
A: Generally, the attendants who arrive with you are the ones
authorized to stay. Changing an attendant while in India is a complex
process and is usually only considered in extreme circumstances or
emergencies, requiring fresh documentation and approval from the
FRRO.
Study in India: Student Visa FAQ
Q. What kind of visa do I need to study in India?
A: If you are pursuing a full-time, structured on-campus course or
research at a government-recognized institution, you must obtain a
Student Visa.
Q. Is there a specific visa for studying Yoga or Indian culture?
A: Yes. If you intend to study Yoga, Vedic culture, Indian dance,
music, or Buddhist studies at an approved institution, you are
required to hold a Student (S-3) Visa.
Q. I am coming to India for an internship. Which visa should I
apply for?
A: Foreigners joining internships at Indian companies, educational
institutions, NGOs, or foreign missions must obtain a Student (S-6)
Visa.

Q: Are there any types of institutions I should avoid?
A: Yes. You must not enroll in courses offered by:
• Franchise educational institutes running off-campus centers.
• Study centers of Open Universities offering distance learning.
• Institutes running "outreach" programs. Always ensure your
institution is duly recognized by the Government of India.
Q. Can I work or start a business while on a Student Visa?
A: No. Foreigners on a Student Visa are strictly prohibited from
engaging in any employment or business activities in India.
Q. Do I need to register with the FRRO?
A: If your Student Visa is valid for more than 180 days (or as per
specific stay stipulations on your visa), you must register with the
concerned FRRO within 14 days of your arrival. Note: Pakistan
nationals have different, stricter registration rules.

Q: What are the financial requirements for students in India?
A: Foreign students are required to open a Non-Resident Ordinary
(NRO) Account at a recognized bank in India. You must then furnish
these account details to the concerned FRRO.

Q: Can I change my college or university after I arrive?
A: A change of institution is generally only allowed under specific
circumstances, such as:
• Insufficient infrastructure or facilities.
• Unavailability of the chosen course. You must provide
reasonable grounds to the authorities for such a request.

Q: What happens if I stop attending my course?
A: If you discontinue your course for any reason, you must intimate
the concerned FRRO immediately.

Q: Are there any travel restrictions for students?
A: If your educational institution is located in a designated Restricted
or Protected Area, you must obtain a RAP/PAP permit before traveling
to that location.

Q: What is the "Study in India" (SII) scheme?
The SII scheme is a government initiative to help foreign students
apply to top-tier Indian institutes. You can find more details and
register at https://studyinindia.gov.in.
TOURISM IN INDIA: Frequently Asked Questions

Q: When should I apply for a Tourist Visa?
A: You should apply for a Tourist Visa if your sole objective for visiting
India is:
• Recreation or sightseeing.
• A casual visit to meet friends or relatives.
• Attending a short-term yoga program (up to 6 months).
• Short-duration medical treatment (including Indian systems of
medicine).

Q: I am traveling with a large group. Is there a specific visa
for us?
A: Yes. If you are traveling in a group of 4 or more members through
a recognized Travel Agency, you may be eligible for a Group Tourist
Visa.

Q: How long can I stay in India on a Tourist Visa?
A: The maximum aggregate stay for a foreigner in a single calendar
year (whether on a regular Tourist Visa, an e-Tourist Visa, or both)
cannot exceed 180 days. Additionally, you must adhere to the
specific continuous stay stay-stipulation limit endorsed on your visa
upon arrival.

Q: Can I extend my Tourist Visa or change it to another type?
A: No. An e-Tourist or regular Tourist Visa is non-extendable and
non-convertible. Once the visa expires or your permitted stay is
over, you must depart the country.

Q: Can I participate in tourist activities if I am on a different
type of visa?
A: Yes. Foreign nationals (except Pakistani nationals) who arrive in
India on any other type of visa (such as a Business or Student visa)
are permitted to engage in activities typically allowed under a Tourist
Visa.

Q: What counts as a "short-term yoga programme"?
A: A short-term yoga program is defined as one that does not exceed
6 months in duration and does not result in the issuance of a formal
qualifying certificate or diploma.

Q: Is there a mandatory gap between two visits?
A: For most travelers, there is no longer a gap requirement. However,
a gap of at least 2 months is mandatory between two visits for
nationals of the following countries:
• Afghanistan, China, Iraq, and Sudan.
• Foreigners of Pakistani origin and stateless persons.

5. What is the difference between “Visa validity/Visa expiry
date” and “Stay stipulation”?
Ans: Visa validity or Visa expiry date refers to the validity period
during which the foreigner can stay in India on the strength of Visa
subject to stay stipulations.
Stay stipulations are the rules which determine the number of days a
foreigner may be permitted to stay in India on the strength of Visa.
Stay stipulations are of two kinds 1) Continuous stay in India from
the date of last arrival. 2) Total Aggregate stay in India during the
calendar year.

🧑 You: pnr of india

🤖 AI:
What are the limitations for OCI cardholders in India? While OCI holders enjoy many benefits, there are certain restrictions:
• Voting: You are not entitled to vote in Indian elections.
• Public Office: You cannot hold constitutional posts (President, Vice-President, Judge of the Supreme Court/High Court) or be a member of Parliament or State Legislatures.
• Employment: You are generally not eligible for appointment to public services or government posts.
• Property: You cannot acquire agricultural or plantation properties in India.

An OCI Cardholder is a foreign national who is officially registered by the Government of India under Section 7A of The Citizenship (Amendment) Act, 2015. The scheme was created to help the Indian Diaspora maintain a strong connection with India through benefits like hassle-free travel and a friendly investment regime.

Chatbot

🧑 You: eta

🤖 AI:
ETA is an Electronic Travel Authorization. It allows the bearer access into the issuing country (India). ETA is NOT eVisa. When the bearer of ETA is allowed entry into India by the Immigration Officer, it becomes eVisa. eVisa will be stamped on the Passport of the foreigner at the designated ICPs on production of valid ETA.

🧑 You: SPECIAL PERMISSIONS REQUIRED BY OCI CARDHOLDERS

🤖 AI:
The OCI Cardholders shall be required to obtain a special
permission or a Special Permit, as the case may be, from the
competent authority or the Foreigners Regional Registration Officer
or the Indian Mission concerned, if they intend to undertake the
following:
i. Research.
ii. Any missionary or Tabligh or Mountaineering or Journalistic
activities;
iii. Internship in any foreign Diplomatic Missions or foreign
Government organizations in India or to take up employment in
any foreign Diplomatic Missions in India;
iv. To visit any place which falls within the Protected or Restricted
or Prohibited areas as notified by the Government of India
Points to remember:
OCI Cardholders who are staying aboard and intend to visit India for
Research activities are advised to obtain the Special Permit from the
concerned Indian Mission abroad before commencing Research in
India.
Please visit www.mha.gov.in for more info
Traveling to India: General Guidelines

When the bearer of ETA is allowed entry into India by the Immigration Officer, it becomes eVisa. eVisa will be stamped on the Passport of the foreigner at the designated ICPs on production of valid ETA.

Q. Is the OCI card a replacement for my national passport?
A: No. Your foreign passport remains your primary travel
document. The OCI card is technically a life-long visa, not a travel
document itself. You must carry both your valid national passport and
your OCI card at all times while traveling to or staying in India.

While OCI holders enjoy many benefits, there are certain restrictions: • Voting: You are not entitled to vote in Indian elections. • Public Office: You cannot hold constitutional posts (President, Vice-President, Judge of the Supreme Court/High Court) or be a member of Parliament or State Legislatures. • Employment: You are generally not eligible for appointment to public services or government posts. • Property: You cannot acquire agricultural or plantation properties in India.

Q: What is an Overseas Citizen of India (OCI) Cardholder?
A:
An OCI Cardholder is a foreign national who is officially registered by
the Government of India under Section 7A of The Citizenship
(Amendment) Act, 2015. The scheme was created to help the Indian
Diaspora maintain a strong connection with India through benefits
like hassle-free travel and a friendly investment regime.

Q: What is "Emigration Clearance" and who needs it?
A: Emigration Clearance is a legal requirement under the Emigration
Act, 1983. It is mandatory for Indian passport holders in the ECR
(Emigration Check Required) category who are traveling to specific
countries for employment.

🤖 AI:
ETA is an Electronic Travel Authorization. It allows the bearer access into the issuing country (India). ETA is NOT eVisa. When the bearer of ETA is allowed entry into India by the Immigration Officer, it becomes eVisa. eVisa will be stamped on the Passport of the foreigner at the designated ICPs on production of valid ETA.

Chatbot

🧑 You: i'm a afghanisthan national i would like to start business in india what are the types of visa

🤖 AI:
Q: I’m just passing through India to another country. Do I
need a Transit Visa?
A: Yes, if your sole purpose is to travel through India to a destination
outside the country, a Transit Visa is required. However, there are
specific exceptions for air and sea travelers who stay within
designated areas.

Q: How long is a Transit Visa valid for?
A: Ordinarily, it is valid for a single journey and must be used for
entry within 15 days from the date it was issued. Note that its validity
will never exceed the validity of the visa you hold for your final
destination country.

Q: How long can I stay in India on a Transit Visa?
A: A Transit Visa is only for "direct transit." This means each visit
cannot exceed 3 days. If you plan to stay in India for more than 72
hours, you must apply for an appropriate visa category (like a Tourist
Visa).
Air & Sea Travel Exceptions

Q: I have a layover at an Indian airport. Do I need a visa if I
don't leave the airport?
A: You do not need a Transit Visa if you are in direct transit by air and
remain within the specified precincts of the airport for less than 24
hours. If your layover exceeds 24 hours, or if you need to leave the
airport precincts (e.g., to stay at a hotel outside), a Transit Visa is
required.

Q: I’m traveling by ship. Do I need a visa when we dock at an
Indian port?
A: No, a Transit Visa is not required as long as you do not leave the
ship while it is at the port.

Q: Can I leave my ship to catch a flight from a nearby airport?
A: Yes, but only with prior approval from the competent authority.
You must hold a valid ticket for a scheduled air service and will only
be allowed to leave the ship to proceed directly to the designated
airport.
Important Restrictions & Extensions

Q: What happens if I don't travel within the 15-day validity
period?
A: If you do not perform your journey through India within the validity
period of your Transit Visa, the visa will lapse. You will need to apply
and pay for a fresh Transit Visa.

Q: Can I extend my Transit Visa if my plans change?
A: Generally, no. Transit Visas are not extendable. Extensions are only
granted in extreme emergencies, such as medical illness, inclement
weather, or major strikes/traffic disruptions.

Q: Can I use a Transit Visa to do a quick 4-day tour?
A: No. If you intend to stay in India for more than 3 days for any
reason, you must obtain a visa of the appropriate category, such as
an e-Tourist Visa.
India Transit Regulations

Q: I have a layover in India. Do I always need a Transit Visa?
A: Not necessarily. If you are traveling by air and staying inside the
airport's transit area for less than 24 hours, you do not need a visa.
However, if your layover is longer than 24 hours or you plan to leave
the airport, you must have a Transit Visa.

Q: How long can I actually stay in the country on a Transit
Visa?
A: A Transit Visa is strictly for short stops. You can stay for a
maximum of 3 days (72 hours) per visit. If you plan to be in India for
more than 3 days, you’ll need to apply for a different category, like a
Tourist Visa.

Q: I am traveling by sea. Do I need a visa when the ship stops
at an Indian port?
A: If you stay on board the ship while it is docked, you do not need a
visa. If you intend to leave the ship to catch a flight, you will need a
visa and prior approval from the competent authorities to proceed to
the airport.

Q: How long is the Transit Visa valid once it is issued?
A: Generally, a Transit Visa is valid for a single journey and must be
used within 15 days of being issued. Also, keep in mind that its
validity cannot exceed the validity of the visa you hold for your final
destination country.

Q: My flight was delayed due to bad weather. Can I extend my
Transit Visa?
A: Normally, Transit Visas cannot be extended. However, exceptions
are made for extreme emergencies such as inclement weather,
strikes, or medical illness. Outside of these rare cases, the 3-day limit
is firm.

Q: What happens if I don't use my Transit Visa within the 15-
day window?
A: If you don't perform your journey within that period, the visa
becomes invalid. You cannot renew it; you would have to apply and
pay for a fresh Transit Visa.

Q: Can I leave the airport to stay at a hotel for two nights on
this visa?
A: Yes! As long as your stay is under 3 days, a Transit Visa allows you
to leave the airport precincts. If your hotel stay or visit exceeds 3
days, you must obtain an appropriate category visa, such as a Tourist
Visa, before you arrive.

Time Duration: 98.57ms

Saved Time:
2026-06-05 17:03:02
